\{X_{11}^{\otimes},~X_{12}^{\otimes},~X_{13}^{\otimes},~X_{14}^{\otimes}\}
earlier, demanded that the worldsheet coordinates
\eta_{I}
in exponent in the amplitude were positive, and obtained a particular box diagram via tropicalization. There are eight such linearly independent sets of propagators
\{X_{I}\}
corresponding to eight box diagrams in the combinatorial polytope
M_{4}
, as depicted in Figure
15
. Repeating the same exercise with any such set, and restricting to a particular region in the moduli space yields a particular box diagram. The list of all eight diagrams appearing in the different regions of the moduli space is given in Table
1
. We illustrate the idea via one more detailed example:
\{X_{24}^{\otimes},~X_{12}^{\otimes},~X_{13}^{\otimes},~X_{14}^{\otimes}\}~.
The string amplitude (
75
) written in the basis
\{X_{24}^{\otimes},~X_{12}^{\otimes},~X_{13}^{\otimes},~X_{14}^{\otimes}\}
is as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{\text{Mob}}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}t\,\int_{0}^{2it}\prod_{r=1}^{3}\mathrm{d}\zeta_{r}\int\mathrm{d}^{d}l\,e^{-\pi t\alpha^{\prime}\left\{(1-\nu_{1})X_{24}^{\otimes}+(\nu_{2}-1)X_{12}^{\otimes}+(\nu_{3}-\nu_{2})X_{13}^{\otimes}+(1+\nu_{1}-\nu_{3})X_{14}^{\otimes}\right\}}

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\times\left(\frac{\theta_{1}(\zeta_{12})\,\theta_{1}(\zeta_{34})}{\theta_{1}(\zeta_{13})\,\theta_{1}(\zeta_{24})}\,e^{2\pi t(\nu_{3}-\nu_{2})}\right)^{\alpha^{\prime}X_{13}/2}\left(\frac{\theta_{1}(\zeta_{23})\,\theta_{1}(\zeta_{14})}{\theta_{1}(\zeta_{13})\,\theta_{1}(\zeta_{24})}\,e^{-2\pi t(1-\nu_{1})}\right)^{\alpha^{\prime}X_{24}/2}~.

(81)

Let us restrict ourselves to the following configuration of the marked points, such that the coefficients of
X_{ik}^{\otimes}
in the exponent are positive, and eventually turn into Schwinger parameters:

Feynman diagrams appearing out of moduli space of Möbius
4
. Second column is the exponent in the amplitude (
75
) expressed in terms of
X_{ik}^{\otimes}
in the triangulation. Third coulmn shows the configuration of marked points on the Möbius strip boundary. Fourth column shows the resultant box Feynman diagram with all external momenta incoming, and
P_{ik}^{\otimes}=l+(p_{1}+\ldots+p_{i-1})+(p_{1}+\ldots+p_{k-1})~.

Changing the integration variables to
\eta^{\prime}_{I}
, proceeding just like the earlier example, we have:

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{\text{Mob}}

\displaystyle\supset\int_{0}^{\infty}\prod_{I\in T^{\prime}}\mathrm{d}\eta^{\prime}_{I}\int\mathrm{d}^{d}l\,e^{-\pi\alpha^{\prime}\sum_{I}(\eta^{\prime}_{I}X_{I}^{\otimes})}\times\left(\frac{\theta_{1}(\zeta_{12})\,\theta_{1}(\zeta_{34})}{\theta_{1}(\zeta_{13})\,\theta_{1}(\zeta_{24})}\,e^{2\pi t(\nu_{3}-\nu_{2})}\right)^{\alpha^{\prime}X_{13}/2}

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt\times\left(\frac{\theta_{1}(\zeta_{23})\,\theta_{1}(\zeta_{14})}{\theta_{1}(\zeta_{13})\,\theta_{1}(\zeta_{24})}\,e^{-2\pi t(1-\nu_{1})}\right)^{\alpha^{\prime}X_{24}/2}~.

(83)

Taking the tropical limit:
\eta^{\prime}_{I}\to\eta^{\prime}_{I}/\alpha^{\prime}
and
\alpha^{\prime}\to 0~,
the factors involving
X_{13}
and
X_{24}
drops out
11
11

11

Keeping in the mind the identification
\zeta=2it\sim 0
, in the
\{X_{24}^{\otimes},~X_{12}^{\otimes},~X_{13}^{\otimes},~X_{14}^{\otimes}\}
configuration, we have
\zeta_{23}=2it-i\eta^{\prime}_{13}~,\zeta_{14}=i(t+\eta^{\prime}_{24})~,\zeta_{13}=i(t+\eta^{\prime}_{14})~,\zeta_{24}=i(t+i\eta_{12}^{\prime})~,
and so the limit of the ratio of theta functions is modified. For instance,

\displaystyle\frac{\theta_{1}(\zeta_{23})\,\theta_{1}(\zeta_{14})}{\theta_{1}(\zeta_{13})\,\theta_{1}(\zeta_{24})}\to\frac{\sin(\pi\zeta_{23})\,\sin(\pi\zeta_{14})}{\sin(\pi\zeta_{13})\,\sin(\pi\zeta_{24})}\to\frac{e^{\pi(2t-\eta^{\prime}_{13})}\,e^{\pi(t+\eta_{24}^{\prime})}}{e^{\pi(t+\eta_{14}^{\prime})}\,e^{\pi(t+\eta_{12}^{\prime})}}\to e^{2\pi\eta^{\prime}_{24}}=e^{2\pi t(1-\nu_{1})}~.

(84)

, and we are left with the box diagram:

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{\text{Mob}}

\displaystyle\supset\int_{0}^{\infty}\prod_{I\in T^{\prime}}\mathrm{d}\eta^{\prime}_{I}\int\mathrm{d}^{d}l\,e^{-\pi\alpha^{\prime}\sum_{I}(\eta^{\prime}_{I}X_{I}^{\otimes})}~.

(85)

In this case, one can interpret the degenerated worldsheet as a box diagram with twists in three propagators, as shown in Table
1
.

Obtaining triangles and bubbles:

The triangle and bubble graphs correspond to the regions in the moduli space where two marked points come close to each other. One can parameterize the moduli space integral appropriately to make such graphs manifest. However, we have been analyzing type-I superstring amplitudes and their field theory limit is the
\mathcal{N}=4
SYM, and famously, the one-loop amplitudes of
\mathcal{N}=4
SYM do not have any triangle or box contributions. We illustrate the idea via an example, and then briefly discuss how the triangles and bubbles appear in non-supersymmetric one-loop string amplitudes.

Consider the region of the moduli space of Möbius strip with four insertions such that
1\leq\nu_{1}\leq\nu_{2}\leq\nu_{3}\leq\nu_{4}=2~,
as depicted in (
77
). The contribution to the amplitude from this region is given by (
78
):

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{\text{Mob}}[1234]

\displaystyle\supset\int_{0}^{\infty}\prod_{I\in T}\mathrm{d}\eta_{I}\int\mathrm{d}^{d}l\,e^{-\pi\alpha^{\prime}\sum_{I}(\eta_{I}X_{I}^{\otimes})}\,\mathfrak{F}(\eta_{I})~,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{F}(\eta_{I})

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\theta_{1}(\zeta_{12})\,\theta_{1}(\zeta_{34})}{\theta_{1}(\zeta_{13})\,\theta_{1}(\zeta_{24})}\,e^{2\pi t(\nu_{3}-\nu_{2})}\right)^{\alpha^{\prime}X_{13}/2}\left(\frac{\theta_{1}(\zeta_{23})\,\theta_{1}(\zeta_{14})}{\theta_{1}(\zeta_{13})\,\theta_{1}(\zeta_{24})}\right)^{\alpha^{\prime}X_{24}/2}~.

(86)

Recall that
I\in\{11,12,13,14\}~.
Let us change variables appropriately such that the triangle graph
\{X^{\otimes}_{11},~X^{\otimes}_{12},~X^{\otimes}_{13},~X_{13}\}
is made manifest as well:

\displaystyle e^{-\pi\eta_{14}}\equiv\frac{e^{-\varphi}}{1+e^{-\varphi}}~,\qquad\Rightarrow\quad\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}\eta_{14}=\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}\varphi\,\frac{1}{1+e^{-\varphi}}~.

(87)

Taking the tropical limit for
\eta_{11}~,\eta_{12}~,\eta_{13}
, but keeping
\eta_{14}
arbitrary, one obtains
\mathfrak{F}
to be dependent only on
\eta_{14}
:

\displaystyle\mathfrak{F}(\eta_{14})=\left(\frac{1+2e^{-\varphi}}{(1+e^{-\varphi})^{2}}\right)^{\alpha^{\prime}X_{13}/2}~.

(88)

Note that in the tropical limit of
\eta_{14}~,
we revert back to
\mathfrak{F}(\eta_{14}\to\infty)\to 1~.
So, the contribution to the amplitude is as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{\text{Mob}}[1234]

\displaystyle\supset\int_{0}^{\infty}\prod_{I\in T}\mathrm{d}\eta_{I}\int\mathrm{d}^{d}l\,e^{-\pi\alpha^{\prime}(\eta_{11}X_{11}^{\otimes}+\eta_{12}X_{12}^{\otimes}+\eta_{13}X_{13}^{\otimes})}

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt\times\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}\varphi}{(1+e^{-\varphi})}\left(\frac{e^{-\varphi}}{1+e^{-\varphi}}\right)^{\alpha^{\prime}X^{\otimes}_{14}}\left(\frac{1+2e^{-\varphi}}{(1+e^{-\varphi})^{2}}\right)^{\alpha^{\prime}X_{13}/2}

(89)

In these coordinates, the region
\varphi\to\infty
has the integrand
e^{-\varphi X_{14}^{\otimes}}
, and the
\mathfrak{F}
does not contribute, and we have the box diagram. In the region
\varphi\to-\infty
, we have
e^{+\varphi X_{13}}
contributing, while the
X_{14}^{\otimes}
term becomes 1. In this tropical limit
\varphi\to\varphi/\alpha^{\prime}
and
\alpha^{\prime}\to 0~,
the leading exponential survives and the exact factors of
\pm 2
become irrelevant. This is precisely how the piecewise linear headlight functions appear as the tropical limit of the string amplitudes. In the integration region
\varphi\to\infty
, with the
X_{13}
propagator turned on, the two insertions
\zeta_{3}
and
\zeta_{4}
are coming close, and we are to get a triangle diagram. However, the integration measure contains
1/(1+e^{-\varphi})
, and it makes the triangle contribution zero. If we were dealing with a bosonic open string theory with tachyons as external particles, the extra factor in the measure would be absorbed into the exponent containing
X_{13}
. In that case, we obtain triangles and bubbles in the similar manner via tropicalization and isolating the leading exponent.

Figure 15:
A portion of the combinatorial polytope
M_{4}
showing the
eight
vertices (complete triangulations) corresponding to eight
box
diagrams in the moduli space of a Möbius strip with four marked points, as shown in Figure 8.13 of
Green:1987mn
. The four gray triangulations are triangle graphs, and more triangulations are omitted. Look at Table
1
to see dual box diagrams.

7
Discussion

We have studied the scattering amplitudes for scalars transforming as the adjoint of
so(n)
or
sp(n),
leading to the non-orientable surfaces. We constructed the usual surfaceology structures by embedding the non-orientable surface into a doubled orientable surface, and projecting the information from the doubled surface. We saw that, analogous to the orientable surfaces, we can construct the appropriate
g
-vectors, headlight functions, and associate momenta to the curves. Similar constructions are possible for higher loops as well. We have explicitly worked out the two-loop case, enumerated the possible curves, associated momenta, and written down the surface Symanzik polynomials. The generalization of spanning trees for surfaces allows us to write down the Symanzik polynomials for higher loop surfaces directly. There are many possible open questions that we point out in this section.

A major question is: what exactly is the curve integral formula calculating? It is calculating an entity, which generically has bubbles and tadpoles on its external legs. In QFT, one has to perform the LSZ reduction to obtain the on-shell scattering amplitude from such an object. The bubbles and tadpoles on the external legs lead to the mass renormalization for QFT and string theory (unless the mass is protected due to supersymmetry or gauge invariance). In string theory, for generic amplitudes receiving mass renormalization, one can use the string field theory framework, decompose the moduli space into different ’Feynman diagrams,’ and deal with the divergent diagrams separately
Pius:2013sca
;
Pius:2014iaa
. In the curve integral, we do not wish to divide up the moduli space of graphs into a sum over graphs, because that would be equivalent to the usual QFT route. We can, however, put an appropriate factor in the curve integral, similar to the MCG fixing kernel, which
turns off
all the cones with bubbles and tadpoles on the external legs, similar in spirit to the decapitation of tadpoles in
Banerjee:2025hnz
. We can argue that the external legs in the curve integral have physical renormalized masses, have no tadpoles or bubbles on external legs, resulting in sensible QFT amplitudes. However, further investigations regarding the renormalization of the curve integral are needed. For instance, see
Banerjee:2025hnz
.

In Section
6
, we took a field theory limit of the Möbius strip superstring amplitude, and obtained the
1/N
subleading contribution to the one-loop amplitude of
SO(N)

\mathcal{N}=4
SYM. It is a sum of eight diagrams, and one can identify the same loop momentum across different non-planar Feynman diagrams using the technology developed. It reinforces the idea about the curve integral, facilitating the choice of a common loop momentum across Feynman diagrams for non-planar processes. The curve integral has potential for efficient writing of the full loop integrand for non-planar amplitudes in
\mathcal{N}=4
SYM. We will explore these in future works.

In this article, we considered the field theory limit of the string amplitudes, but did not construct explicit binary geometry coordinates
u_{I}
for the worldsheet, as done in
Arkani-Hamed:2019_binary_geometry
;
Arkani-Hamed:2024pzc
. It is still an open question whether the complete string loop amplitudes can be computed using the surfaceology and combinatorics. In
Arkani-Hamed:2024pzc
, the authors have defined a surface function
G
closely related to the matrix model correlators, and it will be interesting to construct such surface functions for non-orientable surfaces and to see if one can discover some novel features.

One can spin up the scalar global Schwinger integral into a gluon scattering amplitude, using the Corolla differential operators
Laddha:2024qtn
. In
Arkani-Hamed:2023-HiddenZeros
;
Arkani-Hamed:2023-ScalarScaffolding
;
Arkani-Hamed:2024nhp
, it was discovered that certain deformations of the
\text{Tr}\phi^{3}
amplitudes yield NLSM and YM amplitudes. It would be interesting to see if similar constructions work for the non-orientable surfaces. We will explore this in future works.

Appendix A
Review of combinatorics of the curve integral formula

In this section, we briefly review the combinatorics of the curve integral formula. Refer to
Arkani-Hamed:2023CurveIntegral
;
Arkani-Hamed:2023Multiplicity
for details. Let
S
be a surface with at least one boundary component and
n
marked points on the boundaries. We can triangulate the surface
S
by drawing curves that either end at the marked points, wind around the boundary components with no marked points, or are closed. Note that we are referring to the equivalence class of homotopically equivalent paths on
S
as the
curve/chord
. Below, we describe how to associate a fixed momentum
P_{C}
,
\vec{g}_{C}
, and the headlight function
\alpha_{C}
to each curve
C
.

Let us choose an arbitrary triangulation
T_{0}
of
S
as the reference. Let us draw the dual trivalent graph
\Gamma
, label the internal legs as
\{t_{i}\}
, and label the momenta on all its legs. For higher genus surfaces, certain momenta are loop momenta. Let us denote the path of a curve
C
on the graph
\Gamma
as a series of left and right turns. For instance, (
90
) depicts the path of curve
C_{14}
on a reference triangulation of the disk with five marked points.

\displaystyle\hbox to203.12pt{\vbox to72.51pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 56.7454pt\lower-27.6443pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}

(90)

Momentum:

Using a reference triangulation
T_{0}
, we can associate the following momentum to every curve
C
:

\displaystyle P_{C}=P_{\text{initial}}+\sum_{\text{right turns}}P_{\text{from left}}~.

(91)

For instance, for
C_{14}
in (
90
), considering
p_{1}
as the initial point,
P_{14}=p_{1}+p_{2}+p_{3}~.
Considering
p_{4}
as the starting point, we obtain an overall negative sign. One can check that for
S
being a disk, the momentum squared associated to a curve
C_{ij}
is
X_{ij}=P_{ij}^{2}=(p_{i}+\ldots+p_{j-1})^{2}~.
The momentum associated with a curve is independent of the reference triangulation
T_{0}~.

g vectors:

Let
E
be the number of chords used to completely triangulate
S
. One can associate a unique
g
-vector
g_{C}\in\mathbb{R}^{E}
with every curve
C
by drawing oriented quiver paths inside the reference triangulation, as reviewed in
Laddha:2024qtn
. We review the combinatorics described in
Arkani-Hamed:2023CurveIntegral
. Let
\{t_{1},\,\ldots,\,t_{E}\}
be the labels of internal edges of the graph dual to
T_{0}
. For every instance of
L\,t_{i}\,R
in the path traversed by
C
, we include
+1
in the
t_{i}
entry of the
E
-dimensional
g
-vector, and for every instance of
R\,t_{i}\,L
, we include
-1
. For instance, the curve
C_{14}
in (
90
) has a single
L\,t_{2}\,R
, and so we have
\vec{g}_{14}=(0,1).
The five curves on the disk with five marked points, and their resultant
g
-vectors are as follows:

\displaystyle C_{13}

\displaystyle:\ \ 1\,L\,t_{1}\,R\,3\qquad

\displaystyle\qquad\vec{g}_{13}

\displaystyle=(1,0)~,

(92)

\displaystyle C_{14}

\displaystyle:\ \ 1\,L\,t_{1}\,L\,t_{2}\,R\,4\qquad

\displaystyle\qquad\vec{g}_{14}

\displaystyle=(0,1)~,

\displaystyle C_{24}

\displaystyle:\ \ 2\,R\,t_{1}\,L\,t_{2}\,R\,4\qquad

\displaystyle\qquad\vec{g}_{24}

\displaystyle=(-1,1)~,

\displaystyle C_{25}

\displaystyle:\ \ 2\,R\,t_{1}\,L\,t_{2}\,L\,5\qquad

\displaystyle\qquad\vec{g}_{25}

\displaystyle=(-1,0)~,

\displaystyle C_{35}

\displaystyle:\ \ 3\,R\,t_{2}\,L\,5\qquad

\displaystyle\qquad\vec{g}_{35}

\displaystyle=(0,-1)~.

The result is the
g
-vector fan shown in Figure
1
. Note that the
g
-vectors depend on the reference triangulation
T_{0}~.

Headlight functions:

The headlight functions
\alpha_{C}
are defined to be dual to the
g
-vectors as
\alpha_{C}(\vec{g}_{C^{\prime}})=\delta_{CC^{\prime}}~.
Let
\{y_{1},\ldots,y_{E}\}
be variables associated with each internal edge of the graph dual to
T_{0}.
Given the path traversed by a curve
C
, let us omit the end-points, and construct the following matrix
M_{C}
using the following replacement:

\displaystyle L\to\left(\begin{matrix}1&0\\
1&1\end{matrix}\right).~,\qquad R\to\left(\begin{matrix}1&1\\
0&1\end{matrix}\right)~,\qquad t_{i}\to\left(\begin{matrix}1&0\\
0&y_{i}\end{matrix}\right)~.

(93)

For instance, the curve
C_{13}
on the disk with five marked points leads to:

\displaystyle C_{13}:\ \ 1\,L\,t_{1}\,R\,3\qquad\to\qquad M_{13}=\left(\begin{matrix}1&0\\
1&1\end{matrix}\right).\left(\begin{matrix}1&0\\
0&y_{1}\end{matrix}\right).\left(\begin{matrix}1&1\\
0&1\end{matrix}\right)=\left(\begin{matrix}1&1\\
1&1+y_{1}\end{matrix}\right)

(94)

Given the matrix
M_{C}=\left(\begin{matrix}M_{C11}&M_{C12}\\
M_{C21}&M_{C22}\end{matrix}\right)
, one obtains the headlight functions via tropicalization as follows:

\displaystyle\alpha_{C}=-\text{Trop}\left(\frac{M_{C12}M_{C21}}{M_{C11}M_{C22}}\right)~.

(95)

Note that Trop(
f
) picks up the leading exponential. For instance, Trop(
1+e^{x}+e^{x+y}
)
=\text{max}\left(0,x,x+y\right)~.
In the example of
C_{13}
, introducing
y_{i}=e^{t_{i}}
, we obtain:

\displaystyle\alpha_{13}=-\text{Trop}\left(\frac{1}{1+y_{1}}\right)=\text{Trop}(0+y_{1})=\text{max}\left(0,t_{1}\right)~.

(96)

Following (
92
), we see that
\alpha_{13}(\vec{g}_{13})=1~,
and
\alpha_{13}(\vec{g}_{C\neq 13})=0~.

Appendix B
Details omitted in the main text

In this section, we present some detailed calcualtions omitted in the main text.

Explicit loop momentum integral for Möbius strip

Let us restrict ourselves to the
t_{0}>0
region, and have consistently
\alpha^{\otimes}=0
. The curve integral for Möbius
n
is as follows:

\displaystyle A_{n}^{\text{Mob}}(\{p_{i}\})=\int_{t_{0}>0}\mathrm{d}^{n}\vec{t}\int\mathrm{d}^{D}l\ e^{-\sum_{i\leq j}\alpha^{\otimes}_{ij}(l+P_{1i}+P_{1j})^{2}+\sum_{i,k}\alpha_{ik}X_{ik}}~.

(97)

Let us carry out the loop integration:

\displaystyle\int\mathrm{d}^{D}l\ e^{-\sum_{i\leq j}\alpha^{\otimes}_{ij}(l+P_{1i}+P_{1k})^{2}}

\displaystyle=\int\mathrm{d}^{D}l\ e^{-\sum_{i\leq j}\alpha_{ij}^{\otimes}\big(l^{2}+2l.(P_{1i}+P_{1j})+(P_{1i}+P_{1j})^{2}\big)}

\displaystyle\equiv\int\mathrm{d}^{D}l\ e^{-\mathcal{U}\left[l^{2}+2l.\frac{1}{\mathcal{U}}\sum_{i\leq j}(P_{1i}+P_{1j})\alpha_{ij}^{\otimes}+\frac{1}{\mathcal{U}}\sum_{i\leq j}(P_{1i}+P_{1j})^{2}\alpha_{ij}^{\otimes}\right]}

\displaystyle=\int\mathrm{d}^{D}l\ e^{-\mathcal{U}\left[\tilde{l}^{2}-\left(\frac{1}{\mathcal{U}}\sum_{i\leq j}(P_{1i}+P_{1j})\alpha_{ij}^{\otimes}\right)^{2}+\frac{1}{\mathcal{U}}\sum_{i\leq j}(P_{1i}+P_{1j})^{2}\alpha_{ij}^{\otimes}\right]}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\mathcal{U}^{D/2}}\ e^{+\frac{1}{\mathcal{U}}\left(\sum_{i\leq j}(P_{1i}+P_{1j})\alpha_{ij}^{\otimes}\right)^{2}-\sum_{i\leq j}(P_{1i}+P_{1j})^{2}\alpha_{ij}^{\otimes}}

\displaystyle\equiv\frac{1}{\mathcal{U}^{D/2}}\ e^{-\mathcal{F}/\mathcal{U}}~.

(98)

We introduced
\mathcal{U}=\sum_{i\leq j}\alpha_{ij}^{\otimes}
in the second equality, and
\mathcal{F}
in the last equality:

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}

\displaystyle=-\left(\sum_{i\leq j}(P_{1i}+P_{1j})\alpha_{ij}^{\otimes}\right)^{2}+\mathcal{U}\sum_{i\leq j}(P_{1i}+P_{1j})^{2}\alpha_{ij}^{\otimes}

\displaystyle=-\sum_{i\leq j}\sum_{k\leq l}(P_{1i}+P_{1j}).(P_{1k}+P_{1l})\alpha_{ij}^{\otimes}\,\alpha_{kl}^{\otimes}+\sum_{i\leq j}\sum_{k\leq l}(P_{1i}+P_{1j})^{2}\alpha_{ij}^{\otimes}\alpha_{kl}^{\otimes}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\leq j}\sum_{k\leq l}\alpha_{ij}^{\otimes}\alpha_{kl}^{\otimes}\big((P_{1i}+P_{1j})^{2}-(P_{1i}+P_{1j}).(P_{1k}+P_{1l})\big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\leq j}\sum_{k\leq l}\alpha_{ij}^{\otimes}\alpha_{kl}^{\otimes}\left(-X_{ij}+X_{1i}+X_{1j}-X_{1k}-X_{1l}+\frac{1}{2}\big(X_{ik}+X_{jk}+X_{il}+X_{jl}\big)\right)~.

(99)

In the last expression, the coefficient of
\alpha_{ij}^{\otimes}\alpha_{kl}^{\otimes}
is not yet symmetric under
(ij)\leftrightarrow(kl)~.
For instance, there are two terms that contribute to
\alpha_{13}^{\otimes}\alpha_{24}^{\otimes}
, namely
(ij)=(13)~,~(kl)=(24)
and
(ij)=(24)~,~(kl)=(13)
. We simplify further by choosing
i\leq k~,
so that we have:

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}=

\displaystyle\!\!\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\leq k\leq j\\
k\leq l\end{subarray}}\!\!\alpha_{ij}^{\otimes}\alpha_{kl}^{\otimes}\bigg[\left(-X_{ij}+X_{1i}+X_{1j}-X_{1k}-X_{1l}+\frac{1}{2}\big(X_{ik}+X_{jk}+X_{il}+X_{jl}\big)\right)+\bigg((ij)\leftrightarrow(kl)\bigg)\bigg]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\leq k\leq j\\
k\leq l\end{subarray}}\alpha_{ij}^{\otimes}\alpha_{kl}^{\otimes}\big(-X_{ij}-X_{kl}+X_{ik}+X_{jk}+X_{il}+X_{jl}\big)~.

(100)

With the constraints
i\leq k\leq j
and
k\leq l~,
there are two possibilities
i\leq k\leq j\leq l
and
i\leq j\leq k\leq l~.
For the latter case, the curves
C_{ij}^{\otimes}
and
C_{kl}^{\otimes}
are not compatible:

\displaystyle\alpha_{ij}^{\otimes}\alpha_{kl}^{\otimes}=0~,\quad\forall\quad i\leq j\leq k\leq l~.

(101)

So, we obtain the following formula for
\mathcal{F},
with the limits of
i,j,k,l
updated as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{\text{Mob}}=\sum_{i\leq k\leq j\leq l}\alpha_{ij}^{\otimes}\alpha_{kl}^{\otimes}\big(-X_{ij}-X_{kl}+X_{ik}+X_{jk}+X_{il}+X_{jl}\big)~.

(102)

The other two surface Symanzik polynomials are as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}^{\text{Mob}}=\sum_{i\leq k}\alpha^{\otimes}_{ik}~,\hskip 56.9055pt\mathcal{Z}^{\text{Mob}}=\sum_{i,k}\alpha_{ik}X_{ik}~.

(103)

The loop integrated curve integral for the Möbius strip, with the external momentum dependence coming through
X_{ik}
, is as follows:

\displaystyle A_{n}^{\text{Mob}}(\{p_{i}\})=A_{n}^{\text{Mob}}(\{X_{ik}\})=\int_{t_{0}>0}\mathrm{d}^{n}\vec{t}\,\frac{1}{\mathcal{U}^{D/2}}\,e^{-\mathcal{F}/\mathcal{U}+\mathcal{Z}}~.

(104)

Surface Symanzik polynomials obtained from explicit loop integration matches with the ones constructed from spanning subsurfaces.

Explicit
g
-vectors and headlight functions for
n=3
with wheel as reference triangulation

The following are the
g
-vectors for Möbius
3
with the triangle graph
\big(C_{11}^{\otimes},~C_{12}^{\otimes},~C_{31}^{\otimes}\big)
as the reference triangulation:

\displaystyle\vec{g}_{12}

\displaystyle=\big(0,1,-1\big)~~,

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\vec{g}^{\otimes}_{12}

\displaystyle=\big(0,1,0\big)~~,

(105)

\displaystyle\vec{g}_{23}

\displaystyle=\big(-1,0,-1\big)~~,

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\vec{g}^{\otimes}_{23}

\displaystyle=\big(-1,0,0\big)~~,

\displaystyle\vec{g}_{31}

\displaystyle=\big(1,-1,0\big)~~,

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\vec{g}^{\otimes}_{31}

\displaystyle=\big(0,0,1\big)~~,

\displaystyle\vec{g}_{11}

\displaystyle=\big(1,0,-1\big)~~,

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\vec{g}^{\otimes}_{11}

\displaystyle=\big(1,0,0\big)~~,

\displaystyle\vec{g}_{22}

\displaystyle=\big(-1,1,-2\big)~~,

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\vec{g}^{\otimes}_{22}

\displaystyle=\big(-1,1,-1\big)~~,

\displaystyle\vec{g}_{33}

\displaystyle=\big(0,-1,-1\big)~~,

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\vec{g}^{\otimes}_{33}

\displaystyle=\big(0,-1,0\big)~~,

\displaystyle\vec{g}^{\otimes}=\big(0,0,-1\big)~.

The following are the dual headlight functions:

\displaystyle\alpha_{12}

\displaystyle=\text{max}\left(\text{max}\left(0,\,t_{2},\,t_{1}+t_{2}\right)+\text{max}\left(0,\,t_{3},\,t_{3}+t_{2}\right)\ ,\ t_{1}+2t_{2}\right)-\text{max}\left(0,\,t_{2},\,t_{1}+t_{2}\right)

(106)

\displaystyle-\text{max}\left(0,\,t_{3},\,t_{3}+t_{2}\right)~,

\displaystyle\alpha_{23}

\displaystyle=\text{max}\left(0\ ,\ \text{max}\left(0,\,t_{2},\,t_{1}+t_{2}\right)+\text{max}\left(t_{1},\,t_{3},\,t_{1}+t_{3}\right)\right)-\text{max}\left(0,\,t_{2},\,t_{1}+t_{2}\right)

\displaystyle-\text{max}\left(t_{1},\,t_{3},\,t_{1}+t_{3}\right)~,

\displaystyle\alpha_{31}

\displaystyle=\text{max}\left(0,\,t_{1},\,t_{2},\,t_{1}+t_{2}\right)-\text{max}\left(0,\,t_{2},\,t_{1}+t_{2}\right)~,

\displaystyle\alpha_{11}

\displaystyle=2\,\text{max}\left(0,\,t_{2},\,t_{1}+t_{2}\right)+\text{max}\left(0,\,t_{3},\,t_{2}+t_{3}\right)-\text{max}\left(0,t_{2}\right)

\displaystyle-\text{max}\left(0,\,t_{2},\,t_{1}+t_{2},\,t_{3}+2\,\text{max}\left(0,\,t_{2},\,t_{1}+t_{2}\right)\right)~,

\displaystyle\alpha_{22}

\displaystyle=2\,\text{max}\left(0,\,t_{2},\,t_{1}+t_{2}\right)+\text{max}\left(0,\,t_{3},\,t_{2}+t_{3}\right)+\text{max}\left(t_{1},\,t_{3},\,t_{1}+t_{3}\right)

\displaystyle-\text{max}\left(t_{1}+\text{max}\left(0,\,t_{2},\,t_{1}+t_{2}\right)\ ,\ \text{max}\left(0,t_{1}\right)+\text{max}\left(0,t_{2}\right)+\text{max}\left(0,\,t_{2},\,t_{1}+t_{2}\right)+t_{3}\right)

\displaystyle-\text{max}\left(0,\,t_{3},\,t_{2}+t_{3},\,t_{1}+t_{2},\,t_{1}+t_{2}+t_{3}\right)~,

\displaystyle\alpha_{33}

\displaystyle=2\,\text{max}\left(0,\,t_{2},\,t_{1}+t_{2}\right)+\text{max}\left(t_{1},\,t_{3},\,t_{1}+t_{3}\right)-\text{max}\left(0,t_{1}\right)

\displaystyle-\text{max}\left(t_{3}\ ,\ t_{2}+\text{max}\left(0,\,t_{2},\,t_{1}+t_{2}\right)+\text{max}\left(t_{1},\,t_{3},\,t_{1}+t_{3}\right)\right)~,

\displaystyle\alpha^{\otimes}_{12}

\displaystyle=\text{max}\left(0,\,t_{3},\,t_{2}+t_{3}\right)-\text{max}\left(0,t_{3}\right)~,

\displaystyle\alpha^{\otimes}_{23}

\displaystyle=\text{max}\left(t_{1},\,t_{3},\,t_{1}+t_{3}\right)-\text{max}\left(0,t_{3}\right)-t_{1}~,

\displaystyle\alpha^{\otimes}_{31}

\displaystyle=\text{max}\left(0,t_{3}\right)~,

\displaystyle\alpha^{\otimes}_{11}

\displaystyle=\text{max}\left(0,\,t_{3},\,t_{2}+t_{3},\,t_{1}+t_{2}+t_{3}\right)-\text{max}\left(0,\,t_{3},\,t_{2}+t_{3}\right)~,

\displaystyle\alpha^{\otimes}_{22}

\displaystyle=\text{max}\left(0,\,t_{3}\right)+\text{max}\left(t_{1}\ ,\ t_{3}+\text{max}\left(0,t_{1}\right)+\text{max}\left(0,t_{2}\right)\right)-\text{max}\left(0,\,t_{3},\,t_{2}+t_{3}\right)

\displaystyle-\text{max}\left(t_{1},\,t_{3},\,t_{1}+t_{3}\right)~,

\displaystyle\alpha^{\otimes}_{33}

\displaystyle=\text{max}\left(t_{3}+\text{max}\left(0,t_{2}\right)\ ,\ t_{1}+t_{2}+\text{max}\left(0,t_{3}\right)\right)-t_{2}-\text{max}\left(t_{3},\,t_{1}+\text{max}\left(0,t_{3}\right)\right)~,

\displaystyle\alpha^{\otimes}

\displaystyle=\text{max}\left(t_{3}+2\,\text{max}\left(0,\,t_{2},\,t_{1}+t_{2}\right)+\text{max}\left(t_{1},t_{3},t_{1}+t_{3}\right)\ ,\ 2t_{1}+t_{2}\right)-t_{3}

\displaystyle-2\,\text{max}\left(0,\,t_{2},\,t_{1}+t_{2}\right)-\text{max}\left(t_{1},t_{3},t_{1}+t_{3}\right)~.

One can check that
\alpha_{C}\big(\vec{g}_{C}^{\prime}\big)=\delta_{CC^{\prime}}~.

Determining the momenta
P_{ik}^{\otimes}
for Mobius
3
via mutations

Consider a Mobius strip with three marked points, and let us choose
P_{11}\equiv l
as the loop momentum. In the trivalent graph
\{C_{11}^{\otimes},~C_{12}^{\otimes},~C_{13}^{\otimes}\},
it determines the momenta
P_{12}^{\otimes}
and
P_{13}^{\otimes}
by momentum conservation. By considering mutations, we obtain other diagrams with different curves, and we determine the associated momenta using momentum conservation. Figure
16
depicts this. Using this, we find that
P_{ik}^{\otimes}=l+P_{1i}+P_{1k}~.

Evaluation of
g
-vectors for Möbius
2
by embedding it into an annulus.

Determining the
g
-vectors for Mobius
2

We embed the Möbius strip with two marked points into an annulus with two marked points on each boundary, and project out the
g
-vectors accordingly. Figure
17
depicts the calculation. The result is the
g
-vector fan in Figure
4
.
